## 5. Decision

**Chosen** (founder, 2026-10-16, through `/research`) — the spike plan, not the sync design:
- **Spike scope:** the custom protocol below runs through phases 0–5, plus the PowerSync track (A S1–S4) in parallel, so the adopt-vs-build ADR rests on tests. The FSL counsel review is **closed by founder statement (2026-10-18)** (OQ2).
- **Packaging:** P2 — one prototype, `prototypes/local-first-sync-spike/`, with the 1-day `/prototype` cap waived in writing. The founder's words: "I accept the longer timebox in writing: the sync spike tests brief risk 1, the product's top risk; each phase has a pass/fail gate and stops on the first failure."
- **Run count:** N = 3,000 clean randomized runs at each of the phase 2 and phase 3 gates (per-run defect rate below 0.1% at 95%); 30,000 as an unattended stretch campaign.
- **NOT SOURCEABLE gaps:** none blocks the spike; they block the ADRs and implementation, each with its owner in § 9.

The **sync design itself is not decided**: no sync ADR is taken before the spike. Part A asks for S1–S4 first, Part C for S204 and S205, and Part E's phases 0–3 are the evidence that brief risk 1 asks for. The design recommendations below stay **Recommended - pending user decision** until the ADRs.

**Merged recommendation:**
- **The spike's system under test is the custom protocol specified by Parts B–D.** It consists of:
  - business rows, outbox row and per-device sequence counter written in one local `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction with `synchronous=FULL`;
  - a client-generated `command_id`, with one command per business action;
  - a server inbox keyed `(tenant_id, device_id, command_id)`, holding the payload hash and the stored response;
  - a compare-and-set cursor;
  - ordered batches that record a permanent rejection and advance;
  - append-only money facts;
  - the integer billing function.

  These are what the spike must prove, not accepted decisions.
- **Adopt vs build** stays open until A's S1–S4 run. Running them in parallel with the custom-protocol spike is the founder's choice (OQ12).
- **Conflict, feed and multi-device** (C1–C3) and **time and N5** (D1–D3): the per-part recommendations (5.1) go into the ADRs after the spike, subject to the blocking open questions in § 9.
- **Spike packaging (P1–P3) and run count N** are the founder's choice (F438, OQ12).

**Why**: the parts agree on a single core (4.2 item 1), and every money-critical rule sits in our own server code under either engine choice (F5, F7, F8). A spike on the custom protocol therefore tests the part that cannot be bought. PowerSync's S1–S4 answer only whether its download side and queue are worth an external service with an FSL licence and recent advisories (F10, F14).

**Why not the others**: in each part's section 5 (5.1 below). Licence-blocked on the text read: Couchbase Sync Gateway (F28; needs counsel for 4.x, **closed by founder statement (2026-10-18)**).

**Follow-up record**: ADRs after the spike: (1) adopt vs build; (2) write-path protocol; (3) conflict policy, numbering scheme and the Tier 1 multi-device requirements; (4) feed cursor, bootstrap, versioning and push; (5) session-time measurement and session record shape; (6) N5 lease protocol and key custody, with `/license-design`. Also two `/threat-model` runs, `/api-contract`, `/db-migration`, `/observability-plan` and `/cache-design` (the written no-cache decision for the inbox).

### 5.1 Per-part recommendations (copied unchanged)

#### 5.1.1 Part A — adopt side: engines, licences, fit

**Chosen**: none. Recommended - pending user decision (the caller records the final choice).

**Recommendation**: the real contest on the sourced evidence is A (PowerSync) versus I (custom outbox). Do not adopt B, C, D, E or F for the cashier write path. Take the ADR only after spikes S1 to S4 run. If S1 to S4 pass, adopt PowerSync, modelling money and session time as insert-only event rows keyed by a client-generated ID (this is inference: PowerSync uploads row-level PUT operations, F5, so an insert-only event table lets the upload handler dedupe on that key). If any of S1 to S3 fails, build the custom outbox (I). Leaning to A only because it is the one candidate with documented SQLite3MC on JVM Windows X64 (F3, F11), a Postgres source (F9) and steady releases (F1).

**Why**: PowerSync gives checkpointed downloads and a local queue (F6) but leaves exactly-once, validation and conflict rules to our backend handler (F7, F8). The money-critical half of the problem is therefore ours under A as well as under I. A saves download-side and queue plumbing; whether that is worth an external service with two sync-correctness advisories this autumn (F14), a source-available licence (F10; counsel **closed by founder statement (2026-10-18)**) and a blocking FIFO queue (F6) is the founder's call.

**Why not the others**: see the one-line reasons under the options table. G and H are unassessed, not rejected.

**Follow-up record**: ADR needed (yes: adopt versus build is architectural). No `pins.yaml` change is proposed; if A is chosen, the PowerSync Service, Kotlin SDK and sqlite-core versions of F1 and F2 would be proposed as pins at that time.

#### 5.1.2 Part B — write path: idempotency, ordering and atomicity, delivery

**Recommended - pending user decision** (the founder decides through an ADR after the five parts are merged; this part decides nothing):

- **B1: B1-A.** A client-generated random `command_id` per business action, a server inbox keyed `(tenant_id, device_id, command_id)`, a payload hash, and the stored response returned on replay. Retention: do not expire inbox rows on a timer. Dedupe by sequence needs only the cursor (any `seq <= last_applied_seq` is a replay), and the inbox rows are the audit trail that principle 1 requires, so keep them as long as the money records they describe (archive by month later). A TTL sized to N5 grace would be the fallback if storage proves a problem: the grace must then be at least the longest permitted offline period plus the longest retry delay, and the exact N5 length is not yet decided. The Stripe 24-hour window (F118) is the wrong shape for a device offline for days. Tenant scope: tenant id leads every key and index (F110, F128).
- **B2: B2-A.** Per-device gap-free sequence from a counter row inside the business transaction; one command per business action with all of its records; server cursor advanced by compare-and-set in the same PostgreSQL transaction as the effects. Identifiers and sequences are per device, never per branch, so several devices per branch remain possible later. HLC (B2-B) lost because it is unresearched here, cannot detect a missing command and leans on untrusted clocks; arrival order lost because it breaks brief F2 under concurrent retries.
- **B3: B3-A.** Ordered batches, at-least-once, idempotent apply; permanent rejection recorded and skipped past, never silently dropped; full jitter with config-driven numbers (pending S105); restore and clone detection by options (1) plus (3). What principle 1 means for a rejected command: the device already recorded the sale, so the money fact must remain visible on the device, in the inbox as `rejected` with its reason, and in a review queue; it is corrected by a new record, never edited or deleted. The outcome of a review (accept as a late correction, void with reason) is a business decision for the founder (Q4).
- **ADR needed: yes** (sync protocol). Items that depend on other parts: receipt and daily order numbers allocated offline (Q2), the N5 grace length (Q1), conflict policy (other part).

#### 5.1.3 Part C — conflict policy, server-to-device feed, multi-device readiness

**Recommended - pending user decision.** This part decides nothing; `cto` and the founder decide through the ADR.

**Chosen (recommended)**:
- C1: M1 for money facts; S1 for sessions (S2 lease only when a second device ships); B1 for bills; N1 for numbers (device code + local daily counter); K1 for stock (negative allowed, flagged); C-1 for configuration (versioned immutable snapshots stamped on every fact, If-Match for dashboard edits); operation-based reconciliation on the server (F217 pattern) rather than CRDTs or last-writer-wins.
- C2: cursor B (xid8 stamp and snapshot-xmin horizon, shown working in the probe F208) with C as the fallback if the horizon lag from long transactions proves unacceptable; bootstrap E, which also serves re-bootstrap; tombstone window G; versioning H; push J as the always-correct path plus K (SSE nudge) as an optimisation. The client treats a missed heartbeat, a reconnect, wake from sleep and app start as triggers for a pull by cursor.
- C3: adopt rows 1-10 of the table in 4.3 as Tier 1 requirements; they cost little now and each would force a data migration or a protocol break later.

**Why**: the invariants that need coordination are sequential numbers, stock lower bounds and uniqueness of an open session (F211); the rest is append-only and monotonic (F213). With one device per branch each of those has a single writer, so Tier 1 needs no coordination, provided identity, numbers and ownership are device-scoped from day one. The server acts as arbiter and reviewer, not a merge engine.

**Why not the others**: last-writer-wins and whole-bill replace lose writes (principle 1); a serial-id cursor loses rows (F208); gap-free server numbers and escrow make the counter wait (principle 2) or strand rights; CRDTs add a second data model and still leave same-field conflicts to the application (F215), and their primary text was not read (F216); a mandatory-upgrade client stops selling; logical decoding is operationally heavy for two engineers.

**Follow-up record**: ADR yes (conflict policy; cursor scheme; numbering scheme; multi-device Tier 1 requirements). Several recommendations rest on inference and are PENDING spikes (S201-S207); the ADR should not be accepted before S204 and S205 pass.

#### 5.1.4 Part D — session time, N5 trust protocol, clock skew

**Chosen**: Recommended - pending user decision (the caller records the final choice): **D1-B with D1-I**, **D2-B with a backward-at-boot policy chosen between D2-P1 and D2-P2 in `/license-design`**, **D3-B**. D1-C is deferred.

**Why**:
- D1-B + D1-I: the only combination whose elapsed time ignores clock edits, DST and zone changes while the app runs (desktop-note F117, F326), and whose arithmetic is proven identical across four languages (F325). It states honestly where it falls back to the wall clock (after a restart) and tags those segments.
- D2-B: a signed lease whose grace is a duration measured on the device lets the server stay the source of truth and keeps `exp`-on-wall-clock out of the control (F311). EdDSA/Ed25519 is in the JOSE standards (F313), is deterministic (F314) and fits the RFC 8725 rules (F315). Backward-at-boot needs a policy because honest backward jumps exist (F308) and a rolled-back hardware clock on a powered-off PC is otherwise a free extension; P1 vs P2 is a licence-business trade-off for the founder.
- D3-B: "device time is a claim, server receive time is fact" costs one extra stored time per batch and keeps the trail intact; flags feed the brief F3 review without touching the counter.

**What this design defends against a local administrator, and what it does not.** *Defends:* a cashier or curious staff member without administrator rights, and an administrator who only edits the clock (forward or back, running or powered off, subject to S306 and the backward-at-boot policy); edits of the lease (signature); replay of an older lease within the same store (sequence number); a wrong DST or zone rule on the PC (local rules are not used for elapsed time); disagreement between device and server amounts (same integer function). *Does not defend:* an administrator who restores a full disk or VM snapshot from before the grace was consumed, patches or replaces the app, attaches a debugger, or runs the app in a VM whose clock he controls; Keygen says the same of any offline-only scheme (F318). *Reduces, after the fact:* the server sees the lease sequence in use, the skew, the boot-marker changes and the missing sequence numbers at the next sync and flags them; sales recorded after the restored snapshot are lost to the restorer, which is a deterrent, not a control. Brief risk 8's pass signal ("a tampered clock cannot extend grace in tests") is achievable for clock edits; it should be reworded to exclude state rollback by an administrator, which the founder must accept (Q1b).

**Why not the others**: D1-A fails on honest backward jumps, steps and DST; D1-F disagreed in 137 of 5,007 vectors; D1-S awaits a business rule. D2-A and D2-L are defeated by a clock edit or forgery; D2-C is costly and TPM presence is unknown; D2-P3 does not stop the powered-off rollback. D3-A leaves brief F3 blind; D3-C destroys evidence; D3-D blocks the counter against principle 2.

**Follow-up record**: ADR yes (two: time measurement and session record shape; N5 lease protocol and key custody) plus `/license-design` (grace per plan, expiry behaviour, penalty) and `/threat-model` (section 6). Update this note first if implementation differs.

#### 5.1.5 Part E — fault injection, invariants and oracle, spike design

**Chosen**: Recommended — pending user decision (the caller records the final choice).

**Why**: A layered, language-neutral approach: deterministic enumerated failpoints and process kill for the matrix, Toxiproxy for TCP faults (single MIT binary on Windows and Linux), application-layer resend for duplicates and reordering, a bespoke independent oracle, a seeded replay rule, and a mutation self-test that proves the oracle can fail. Windows crash and power-loss cases come last (Phase 4) because no Windows image exists yet. Packaging under the `/prototype` caps is the founder's call (4.6): I do not choose it.

**Why not the others**: Antithesis (price unknown, Linux containers, cannot exercise a Windows app), a self-built FDB-style simulator (weeks), Jepsen/Elle (new Clojure toolchain; checks isolation, not conservation), SQLite-style simulated VFS (duplicates what SQLite already tests), clumsy/WinDivert as primary (Windows 10/11 support not sourced; kernel driver; manual).

**Follow-up record**: ADR needed for the sync protocol (Part B) — not this note. Pin changes: none yet; tools are test-only and not yet pinned (see Proposed pin changes at the end).

##### [E] 5.1 E2 — invariants and test oracle (proposal; derived, not sourced)

All amounts are integer minor units. Time is derived from events by one pure function. The oracle is **independent of the system under test (SUT)**: the harness writes its own **intent log** (every action id it submitted and every acknowledgement it observed) to its own storage *before* submitting; only SUT processes are killed, never the harness.

| ID | Invariant | Oracle check (after faults stop and the system quiesces) |
|----|-----------|---------------------------------------------------------|
| I1 | No acknowledged action is lost | every action the harness saw acknowledged as *locally committed* appears on the server ledger exactly once. The acknowledgement contract must be fixed first: "acknowledged" = durable on the client (for `synchronous=FULL` see desktop-note F106) — Open question Q1 |
| I2 | Each client action id has at most one effect | count of ledger rows (money and time) per (tenant, device, action id) ≤ 1; replays return the original result, never a new effect |
| I3 | Conservation | Σ amounts in the server ledger = Σ amounts of the oracle's accepted events (refunds negative); per bill, Σ payment lines = bill total |
| I4 | Per-device order preserved | for each device, server commit order of applied actions is strictly increasing in device sequence number; policy for gaps (hold vs reject) is Part B/C |
| I5 | Each session minute billed once | billed intervals per session are disjoint; billed time = f(accepted start/stop events) recomputed by the oracle; clock-jump cases defer to Part D |
| I6 | Day-close total equals recomputation | the stored/printed close total on device AND on server = fold over accepted events of that business day |
| I7 | Convergence (liveness, reported separately) | device and server hold the same set of action ids within a bounded time after faults stop; timeout = liveness failure, never a pass |
| I8 | No phantom effects | no server effect for an action id the harness never submitted, nor with a different payload hash than submitted (restored-backup / clone cases) |
| I9 | Local store integrity | `PRAGMA integrity_check` ok after every crash, per F401 practice; backup/copy rules per desktop-note F105–F107 |

##### [E] 5.2 E2 — fault-point coverage matrix for "close and pay a bill"

Steps in order. A fault is injected at each numbered point; every meaningful (point × fault) cell is **enumerated**, not sampled (F402 practice), then single faults are paired (compound, F402).

| Pt | Point (between steps) | Meaningful faults | Expected state after recovery + retry | Invariants at risk |
|----|-----------------------|-------------------|---------------------------------------|--------------------|
| P1 | Before the local commit begins | client kill | no bill closed, nothing acknowledged; user retries | I1 (none acknowledged) |
| P2 | Inside the local commit (before / during / after the commit record is durable but before the call returns) | client kill, client power-off (Phase 4), disk-full / I/O error | either whole bill closed + outbox row, or nothing — never one without the other | I1, I2, I9 |
| P3 | After local commit, before outbox write (**only exists if the outbox row is a separate transaction**) | client kill | the test shows whether the design allows it; recommended design removes this point (bill + outbox row in one local transaction) — to be decided in Part B | I1 (lost money event) |
| P4 | After outbox write, before send | client kill, clock jump | outbox row present; sender resumes | I1, I7 |
| P5 | Mid-send (request partly written) | network cut (request path), client kill, slicer | server sees nothing or a truncated request and ignores it | I2, I8 |
| P6 | Request at server, before server commit | server kill, network cut, duplicate send, concurrent retry | no effect, or exactly one after retry | I2, I3 |
| P7 | Inside the server commit | server kill (incl. database kill), concurrent retry | atomic: whole effect or none | I2, I3, I4 |
| P8 | After server commit, before the response is written | server kill | effect exists once; client retry gets the original result | I2, I3 |
| P9 | Response in flight | network cut (response path), delay beyond client timeout | client unsure; resend produces no second effect | I2, I3 |
| P10 | Response received, before local ack mark | client kill | outbox row still "unsent"; resend is a no-op on the server | I2, I3 |
| P11 | Inside the local ack-mark write | client kill, I/O error | ack mark absent or complete; resend safe | I2, I9 |
| P12 | After the local ack mark | client kill, restore of an older client backup (SC-12) | nothing to resend; older backup re-sends old sequence numbers → must not duplicate | I2, I8 |

Rough cell count: 12 points × ≈ 4 faults ≈ 50 single-fault cells; ≈ 1,000+ pairs (arithmetic estimate, not measured). Each single cell is run repeatedly with different seeds and payloads.

##### [E] 5.3 E2 — number of randomized runs (derived from F431/F432)

- One **run** = a seeded script of K business actions with a randomly drawn fault schedule, followed by quiescence and the full oracle. A run passes only if all of I1–I9 hold.
- Zero violations in n runs bounds the per-run defect probability p below 1−α^(1/n) (F432), **for the fault distribution the generator draws from**. Table (95% unless stated):

| Defect rate to rule out (per run) | Runs needed, zero violations |
|---|---|
| 1 in 100 (1%) | 299 |
| 1 in 200 (0.5%) | 598 |
| 1 in 1,000 (0.1%) | 2,995 (4,603 for 99%; 6,905 for 99.9%) |
| 1 in 10,000 (0.01%) | 29,956 |

- Recommended gates (proposal): Phase 2 and 3 each need ≥ 3,000 clean runs (rules out ≥ 0.1% per-run defects at 95%). A larger unattended campaign of ≈ 30,000 runs rules out ≥ 0.01%. Brief's "N" is therefore proposed as 3,000 (minimum) / 30,000 (stretch); the founder chooses.
- The bound is useless if the generator never reaches a fault class. A defect that needs a kill inside a window of microseconds has near-zero per-run probability under wall-clock random kills → reach it by enumerated failpoints (5.2), not by chance.
- Hours per run are unmeasured (`PENDING — S401`); the number of runs achievable depends on it. Real power cuts (Phase 4) are too few to bound a rate: 0 failures in 20 runs bounds p only to about 14% at 95% (computed from the formula in F432: 1−0.05^(1/20)).
- **Oracle self-test (mutation)**: before counting runs, plant known defects in the SUT stub (outbox in a separate transaction; no server dedupe; ack-before-commit; double billing of a minute) and require the harness to flag each within the planned n. If a mutant survives, the "zero violations" result means nothing.

##### [E] 5.4 E3 — spike phases (each answers one question)

Timeboxes are my proposals, not sourced; they are shown to let the founder see the conflict with the 4-hour / 1-day caps (F438). Hardware and conditions are recorded for every timing number; the Linux dev host is not the reference PC, so timing is "indicative only".

| Phase | Question it answers | Method | Pass signal | Proposed timebox |
|-------|---------------------|--------|-------------|------------------|
| 0 | Can the harness detect deliberately planted bugs? | Naive SUT stub (client store + server) + 4 mutants + oracle | every mutant caught; the correct stub passes; replay by seed reproduces a failure | ≈ 1 day |
| 1 | Does the encrypted local store keep "bill closed + outbox row" atomic under process kill at every point P1–P4, P10–P12? | Process kill at enumerated failpoints; reopen; I1 (local part), I9 | 0 violations over every cell × ≥ 50 seeds | ≈ 1–2 days |
| 2 | With a real server database, does each action have exactly one effect under network cut, resend, duplicate replay and concurrent retry? | Toxiproxy + application-level resend + server kill at P6–P9; I1–I4, I8 | 0 violations in all single-fault cells + ≥ 3,000 randomized runs | ≈ 2–3 days |
| 3 | Do compound faults, reordering, partial batch, restored backup and cloned disk keep the invariants? | Pairs of faults, SC-06, SC-10, SC-12, SC-13; long seeded campaign | 0 violations, ≥ 3,000 runs (stretch 30,000) | ≈ 2–3 days |
| 4 | Does the durability claim survive OS crash / power-off on Windows 10/11? | Hyper-V `-TurnOff` on a Windows VM, then a few real pulls on the 4 GB HDD reference PC; optional clumsy spot-check (S410) | I1/I9 hold in every run; reported as "no defect found in n runs", never as a rate | needs the Windows image (founder, desktop spike phase 0) |
| 5 | Does the day-close total equal the recomputation under clock jumps and session spans? | I5/I6 with Part D scenarios | 0 violations | depends on Part D |

##### [E] 5.5 Harness structure (neutral on language and runtime)

- **Driver** (one process, never killed): seeded RNG → action script + fault schedule; owns the **intent log**; calls the SUT through an **adapter contract** (JSON lines over stdin/stdout or HTTP on 127.0.0.1): `submit(action_id, payload)`, `kill(component, point)`, `restart(component)`, `quiesce()`, `dump_client_state()`, `dump_server_ledger()`, `set_fault(kind, params)`.
- **Fault controllers**: Toxiproxy via its HTTP API; named failpoints via an environment variable or admin call in the SUT (seams compiled only into the prototype); optional tc/netem and dm-flakey on the Linux host.
- **SUT adapters**: one thin adapter per candidate stack, so Compose Desktop / Electron / Tauri and Go / Ktor are tested through the same contract; the harness does not pick the stack.
- **Oracle**: reads the intent log, the server ledger dump, the client dump; applies I1–I9; prints the seed, commit hashes and the first violating action id.
- **Rules**: any failure is reproducible from `seed + SUT commit + harness commit`; a flaky pass is a failure; every run records fault schedule and tool versions. Server database: PostgreSQL 18.6 container (pinned image) so server-side idempotency is real, not simulated.

##### [E] 5.6 Scenario list

| ID | Scenario | Tool | Points | Invariants |
|----|----------|------|--------|------------|
| SC-01 | Network cut mid-close (request path) | Toxiproxy reset_peer/limit_data/timeout; in-process drop | P5–P6 | I1–I3 |
| SC-02 | Response lost after server commit | Toxiproxy on the response direction (to verify S402); in-process drop-after-commit | P8–P9 | I2, I3 |
| SC-03 | Resend after timeout | latency toxic > client timeout | P9 | I2 |
| SC-04 | Duplicate replay of the same request (sequential, burst of 10) | harness resends | P6–P12 | I2, I3 |
| SC-05 | Concurrent retry (two in flight, same action id) | harness | P6–P7 | I2, I3 |
| SC-06 | Reordering of two consecutive actions (2 arrives first) | two connections + delay toxic | P5–P7 | I4 |
| SC-07 | Client crash between local commit and outbox enqueue | failpoint P3 (both transaction designs) | P3 | I1 |
| SC-08 | Server crash after commit, before ack | failpoint/kill at P8 | P8 | I2, I3 |
| SC-09 | Database crash mid-commit | kill the PostgreSQL container | P7 | I2, I3, I4 |
| SC-10 | Partial batch (j of k actions applied, then cut) | failpoint/limit_data | P5–P7 | I2, I4 |
| SC-11 | Clock jump on the client (forward/back) | libfaketime-style or adapter clock; Part D | P4, P10 | I5, I6 |
| SC-12 | Restored client backup replays old sequence numbers (same seq, different content) | restore an older copy of the local store (desktop-note F107 rules) | P12 | I2, I8 |
| SC-13 | Cloned disk (two machines, one device identity) | copy store, run both | P12 | I2, I8 |
| SC-14 | Power loss at commit; lying flush | Hyper-V TurnOff (Phase 4); dm-flakey drop_writes on Linux | P2 | I1, I9 |
| SC-15 | Half-open / sliced connection | Toxiproxy slicer, timeout | P5, P9 | I2 |
| SC-16 | Server restart / deploy during sync | kill + restart | P6–P9 | I2, I7 |
| SC-17 | Ack-mark write fails (disk full / I/O error) | in-process | P11 | I2, I9 |
| SC-18 | Outbox replay after client upgrade / migration | adapter | P4 | I1, I2 |

SC-12/13/18 are my derivations (F441); not sourced.

##### [E] 5.7 Pass / fail criteria

- **PASS (per phase)**: zero violations of the phase's invariants in every enumerated cell and in ≥ the phase's run count; mutation self-test passed; all runs reproducible from seed; the exact tool versions and hardware recorded.
- **FAIL**: any single violation of I1–I6, I8, I9, or a mutant that survives. A failure ends the phase, with the seed kept as a regression case.
- **LIVENESS FINDING (not a pass)**: I7 timeout.
- **NOT ASSESSED**: a phase not run, or run with no one reading the oracle output (`/prototype` rule: unused prototype has no verdict).
- A pass is reported as "no defect found in n runs; per-run defect rate below X at 95% under this generator" — never "correct".

##### [E] 5.8 What the spike must NOT be used to conclude

- That the sync design is correct in general, or that production can skip its own tests (gate 3).
- Anything about durability on real disks from Linux or from process-kill results (F403): only Phase 4 touches OS crash, and only with few runs.
- Anything about NTFS/Windows behaviour from the Linux dev host.
- Multi-device conflicts or LAN behaviour (Tier 1 has one device; Part C).
- Latency, throughput or battery numbers (unrepresentative hardware: "indicative only"); the reference PC is Windows 10/11, 4 GB, slowest CPU, HDD.
- Security: the spike skips auth, tenancy, authorisation, rate limiting and real error handling — these shortcuts must be listed in its README and report. Idempotency is NOT a shortcut; it is the thing under test.
- That a larger N exists "for free": the bound only covers fault types the generator can produce.
- That the chosen desktop runtime or backend language is validated.

##### [E] 5.9 Evidence to retain

Under `prototypes/<name>/` (and referenced from the spike note): the seed list and exact commands; SUT and harness commit hashes; tool versions (Toxiproxy, SQLite, SQLCipher/Multiple Ciphers build, PostgreSQL image digest, OS, kernel); intent log, client dump and server ledger dump for every failing run; the full fault schedule; coverage table of enumerated cells with run counts; mutation self-test report; hardware description for any timing; `SPIKE-NOTE.md` or report with the `Shortcuts taken` list and security surprises. Test keys only; no real secrets or customer data.

##### [E] 5.10 The constraint conflict (F438)

The skill caps (≈ 4 h spike, 1 day concept) and the brief (1–2 weeks) conflict. Options P1–P3 are in 4.6. I do not resolve it; the founder (or `cto` for P3) does.

## 6. Security Notes

- **Controls looked up** (ASVS 5.0.0 official files, F323, F434–F436): V2.2.2, V2.3.1, V2.3.2, V2.3.3, V2.3.4, V2.3.5 (L3), V9.1.1–V9.1.3, V9.2.1–V9.2.4, V11.1.1, V11.1.2, V11.2.1–V11.2.3, V13.3.1, V13.3.4, V14.1.1, V14.1.2, V15.4.2 (L3), V16.2.1, V16.2.2, V16.2.5, V16.3.3, V16.5.3. API Security Top 10 2023 (F324): API1, API2, API4, API6. **Not looked up:** ASVS IDs for authorisation, tenant isolation and anti-automation on the sync endpoints (Parts A, B and C: NOT SOURCEABLE). MASVS 2.1.0 does not apply to a Windows client, and the studio's list has no desktop-client standard (F323).
- **Advisories**:
  - GitHub advisory listing, 2026-10-16 (F14, F18, F32); affected ranges not read:
    - PowerSync service: GHSA-6wfp-847m-28x5 (high), GHSA-hfrf-pvrg-5c3h (medium), GHSA-q6wc-xx4m-92fj (medium).
    - Electric: GHSA-m4f6-p2j8-rq76 (high), GHSA-h5rg-pxx7-r2hj (critical), GHSA-c82q-v86f-c87f (low).
  - OSV, 2026-10-16: none for pgx 5.11.0, ktor-server-core 3.6.0, exposed-core 1.5.1 and coroutines 1.11.0 (F240), nor for the test-tool versions (F437).
  - **Not searched:** PostgreSQL 18.6, SQLite 3.53.4, SQLCipher 4.19.0, SQLite3MC 2.6.1, the Go toolchain, the OTel SDKs and any JWS/EdDSA library (none chosen); a separate GHSA search for Parts B–D; clumsy, WinDivert, Jepsen, Elle, Antithesis and failpoint.
- **Abuse cases** (merged and de-duplicated; per-part detail in 6.1):
  1. A captured batch is replayed to double a payment or refund (B, C, E).
  2. The same command id is sent with an altered payload (F124, E).
  3. A restored backup or cloned disk resends used sequence numbers with different content (F123, SC-12, SC-13).
  4. Cross-tenant id probing through unique-key errors that bypass RLS (F128, F207).
  5. A device enrolled for tenant A sends tenant B ids, or an engine sync-rule bug leaks rows (F14, F18).
  6. Poison commands stall the queue (F6) or flood the review queue (B).
  7. A device skips a sequence number to hide a command; the gap is refused (B).
  8. The clock is rolled back to extend N5 grace or to shorten a running bill (D cases 1–2).
  9. A backdated start (idea-pool S7) with an accomplice approver (C, D).
  10. An older lease is replayed, a lease is forged after key theft, or the algorithm is confused (D).
  11. A downgrade to an older app build (D).
  12. A stolen or cloned device keeps receiving configuration and N5 data until revoked (C).
  13. A revoked permission or deleted item comes back after a long offline gap (F219).
  14. Oversize batches and feed floods; a push nudge reveals that another tenant changed something (C).
  15. Test-tool hazards: an unauthenticated Toxiproxy API off loopback (F411); a kernel packet driver on a machine that holds customer data (E).
- **Not defended against a local administrator** (5.1.4): a full disk or VM snapshot restore, app patching, a debugger, a VM-controlled clock. These are detected only after the fact, at the next sync. Brief risk 8's pass signal must be reworded to exclude them (OQ3).
- **Threat models**: required. Two are proposed: `docs/architecture/threat-models/local-first-sync.md` (sync endpoint, money, staff PII, encrypted outbox) and `docs/architecture/threat-models/n5-lease-and-session-time.md` (Part D). Neither exists yet, and `docs/architecture/` does not exist.
- **Legal (needs counsel; **closed by founder statement (2026-10-18)**)**: the FSL and BSL readings (F10, F28); PDPL residency for vendor clouds (Part A); PDPL for staff clock in/out (F329, Part C); the Egyptian receipt-numbering rule (F241).

### 6.1 Per-part security notes (copied unchanged)

#### 6.1.1 Part A

- **Controls**: ASVS 5.0.0 and MASVS 2.1.0 requirement IDs: NOT SOURCEABLE - the official documents were not fetched in this part (turn budget). The control areas to map once looked up: server-side validation of every uploaded write, tenant isolation of synced data, anti-replay and idempotency, key custody of the local DB (desktop note F112-F116), audit trail. `.claude/docs/security-baseline.md` was not re-read in this part.
- **Advisories** (GitHub published-advisory listing, 2026-10-16; OSV not run): PowerSync service GHSA-6wfp-847m-28x5 (high, WebSocket DoS), GHSA-hfrf-pvrg-5c3h (medium, incorrect results synced with certain JOIN conditions), GHSA-q6wc-xx4m-92fj (medium, some sync filters ignored on 1.20.0 with `config.edition: 3`) (F14). Electric GHSA-m4f6-p2j8-rq76 (high, auth bypass on `/v1/shape`), GHSA-h5rg-pxx7-r2hj (critical, SQL injection), GHSA-c82q-v86f-c87f (low) (F18). None published for the other repositories searched (F32). Affected ranges against the version we would pin must be read at ADR time.
- **Abuse cases**: (1) a client or a replayed network request uploads the same payment or session-close twice; the handler must make the second a no-op (F7). (2) A cashier, or someone with the local key, edits the local database to change an amount or backdate time; the server must treat every uploaded event as untrusted input and validate it (desktop note section 6: the cashier PC is untrusted). (3) A poison write rejected with 4xx stalls the device queue (F6), a self-inflicted outage or a deliberate denial of service. (4) A sync filter or shape misconfiguration, or an engine bug, returns another tenant's rows (F14 sync filters ignored, F18 shape auth bypass). (5) Offline clock manipulation to extend the N5 grace (time handled in another part and the desktop note 4d).
- **Threat model**: required (money, PII, auth/licensing); not yet written: `docs/architecture/threat-models/local-first-sync.md`, owner `security-lead`.
- **Legal**: PowerSync Cloud, Turso Cloud and Capella would host Kodeebo data outside Egypt; PDPL (Law 151/2020) residency and processor terms: needs counsel (**closed by founder statement (2026-10-18)**); primary text not fetched in this part.

#### 6.1.2 Part B

- **Controls**: OWASP ASVS 5.0.0 and API Security Top 10 2023 IDs: NOT SOURCEABLE — turn budget, not reached (F130). Do not copy IDs from memory; `security-lead` or `appsec-engineer` should map them from the official documents. The areas to map are: authentication and token expiry of a long-lived device identity, authorisation of device to tenant, tenant isolation of every key and query, input validation and size limits on batches, anti-replay, logging without secrets or payload PII.
- **Advisories**: not searched (F130). The search for PostgreSQL 18.6, SQLite 3.53.4, SQLCipher 4.19.0, pgx 5.11.0, Ktor 3.6.0 and Exposed 1.5.1 in GHSA and OSV could not run in this part (turn budget); desktop-note F102 records that SQLCipher 4.19.0 fixes two low-risk issues with no CVE listed.
- **Abuse cases** (to feed the threat model): (1) a captured batch is replayed by another party: dedupe returns stored results, but authentication must bind a request to a device; (2) tenant B guesses a command id used by tenant A: with a global key it learns the id exists (F128); with tenant-leading keys it does not; (3) a restored backup or cloned disk issues different commands under used sequence numbers (F123), possibly to resend altered amounts; (4) an altered outbox payload between retries is caught by the payload hash (F124); (5) a flood of oversize or bogus batches: size limits, 413 and 429 (F115), per-device rate limits; (6) a device that keeps sending poison commands to fill the review queue; (7) a device deliberately skipping a sequence number to hide a command: a gap is refused, so the device cannot advance past it; (8) backdated commands: the server must record both device time and receipt time and never trust the device clock for ordering (other part, N5); (9) bearer token expiring mid-batch causing partial application: 401 means not applied (F115), so the unapplied remainder is resent.
- **Threat model**: required. Run `/threat-model` for the sync endpoint (money, authentication, PII of staff attendance, encrypted outbox): `docs/architecture/threat-models/local-first-sync.md`, not yet written.

#### 6.1.3 Part C

- **Controls**: ASVS 5.0.0, MASVS 2.1.0 and API Top 10 2023 requirement IDs: NOT SOURCEABLE - turn budget, not looked up in the official documents; `security-lead` must map them (areas: authorization on every sync endpoint, tenant isolation, replay and idempotency, object-level authorization, validation of device batches).
- **Advisories**: OSV queried 2026-10-16 for pgx 5.11.0, ktor-server-core 3.6.0, exposed-core 1.5.1, coroutines 1.11.0: no advisory returned (F240, tier E). PostgreSQL 18.6 fixed 24 CVEs per the stack reference (`docs/stack-reference/postgres/VERSION.md`, 2026-10-11); no open advisory for 18.6 was searched. GHSA was not searched separately. The go toolchain and OpenTelemetry versions were not queried.
- **Abuse cases**: (1) replaying an old batch or one batch twice to double a payment (dedup key must include tenant and device; brief F2); (2) a device enrolled for tenant A submitting facts with tenant B ids, or probing for another tenant's ids through duplicate-key errors, which bypass RLS (F207); (3) a stolen or cloned device continuing to receive configuration, permissions and N5 data until revoked; (4) backdated sessions or voids used to hide cash, with an accomplice approver; (5) editing the local outbox or database to change amounts (tamper detection of the encrypted file is in desktop note F101; server-side recomputation of totals does not depend on the device); (6) rolling the device clock back to extend offline time or backdate; (7) a feed query run with a role that bypasses RLS leaking another tenant's rows (use the `app` role, F207); (8) a deleted item or revoked permission returning after a long offline gap (F219); (9) oversized batches and feed floods as denial of service; (10) the push channel used to learn that another tenant changed something (the nudge must carry no data and be authenticated per tenant).
- **Threat model**: required (money, PII via staff attendance, licensing). `docs/architecture/threat-models/local-first-sync.md` does not exist yet; run `/threat-model`.
- **Legal**: PDPL treatment of attendance data on the feed: needs counsel (**closed by founder statement (2026-10-18)**), NOT SOURCEABLE here.

#### 6.1.4 Part D

- **Controls** (ASVS 5.0.0, IDs read from the official CSV, F323; applies to the server side and, by analogy only, to the desktop client - MASVS 2.1.0 is mobile and not applicable): V9.1.1, V9.1.2, V9.1.3, V9.2.1-V9.2.4 (lease validation, algorithm allowlist, trusted keys, audience, type); V11.1.1, V11.1.2, V11.2.1-V11.2.3 (key lifecycle, inventory, vetted libraries, crypto agility, >= 128-bit); V13.3.1, V13.3.4 (signing key in a vault, rotation); V2.2.2, V2.3.2 (server-side validation, business-logic limits - skew and backdating limits live on the server); V2.3.5 (L3: multi-user approval for high-value flows - the idea-pool S7 backdated start approver); V14.1.1, V14.1.2, V14.2.4 (classify and protect clock in/out data; V14.2.4 is listed in the CSV read but its text was only skimmed); V16.2.1, V16.2.2, V16.3.3 (log metadata, UTC or explicit offset, log bypass attempts). API Top 10 2023: API2 (lease endpoint auth), API6 (replaying or bulk-submitting sessions), API1 (tenant isolation on sync), API4 (rate limits on lease and sync) (F324). RFC 8725 best practices as the token checklist (F315).
- **Advisories**: could not run - NOT SOURCEABLE (turn budget, not reached). No library or backend language is chosen, so there is no chosen version to search; GHSA and OSV must be searched for each JWS/EdDSA library and the Windows runtime once the stack is decided (Q10, Q13).
- **Abuse cases**:
  1. Cashier sets the Windows clock back (or the BIOS clock while powered off) to extend N5 grace (brief risk 8). Control: monotonic accounting, high-water mark, backward-at-boot policy (D2-P), flag at sync. Not fully closed until S306.
  2. Cashier edits the clock during a customer's session to shorten the bill and pockets the difference. Control: monotonic elapsed (desktop-note F117), `elapsed_source` tag, wall-vs-monotonic divergence flag.
  3. Backdated start (idea-pool S7) used to reduce a bill: approver, reason and actor recorded; limit on how far back is server config; flagged on the day-close review (Q6).
  4. Replay of an old lease from a backup to regain grace: sequence number; server sees the sequence in use; defeated only if the whole state is restored (not defended).
  5. Blocking the server (hosts file, firewall) to stay inside grace: this is the intended offline case; grace is bounded by the lease.
  6. Forged lease after theft of the signing key: key in a vault, `kid` rotation, short validity of keys' use; compromise response = ship a new key and revoke the old one in an update.
  7. Algorithm confusion, `alg: none`, attacker-chosen key URL: allowlist and pinned embedded keys (RFC 8725, ASVS V9.1.2, V9.1.3).
  8. Downgrade to an older app build with older keys or weaker checks: the server refuses sync from versions below a minimum (inferred; Q9).
  9. A PC without the Windows time-zone update converts Egypt DST wrongly: not a security attack, but an integrity defect; avoided by never using PC local rules for elapsed time (F304, F326).
- **Threat model**: required (licensing, crypto, money, PII). Path to create: `docs/architecture/threat-models/n5-lease-and-session-time.md` via `/threat-model`. Time for staff clock in/out is PII-adjacent; classification and retention are "needs counsel" (F329; **closed by founder statement (2026-10-18)**).

#### 6.1.5 Part E

- **Controls (ASVS 5.0.0, looked up — F434–F436)**: 2.3.1 (flow order — a replayed or re-ordered close must not skip steps); 2.3.3 (atomic business transaction — the P2/P7 matrix cells); 2.3.4 (business-level locking/double-booking — concurrent retry SC-05); 15.4.2 (check-and-act atomic — dedupe check and effect in one transaction); 16.3.3 (log attempts to bypass business logic — duplicate/replay detections); 16.5.3 (no fail-open transaction on error). Idempotency/duplicate submission has no ASVS 5.0.0 requirement in the V2 text read → covered by the Part B design and these tests. MASVS 2.1.0 / API Top 10 2023 IDs: NOT SOURCEABLE — not looked up in this part.
- **Advisories**: OSV 2026-10-16, exact versions of the test libraries: none (F437); Toxiproxy GitHub advisories page: none published. clumsy, WinDivert, Jepsen/Elle, Antithesis, failpoint: GHSA search not run.
- **Abuse cases** (feed the threat model): (1) a cashier or attacker replays a captured sync request to double-credit or double-refund; (2) same action id with a different payload to rewrite a past bill; (3) restoring an older client backup or cloning the disk to re-send old sequence numbers; (4) clock rollback to extend billed or free time (Part D / N5); (5) cutting the network at the exact point to force a retry that duplicates a payment; (6) an unauthenticated Toxiproxy HTTP API on a non-loopback address (F411) letting anyone inject faults in a shared test network; (7) a kernel packet driver (WinDivert) on a machine that also holds customer data.
- **Test hygiene**: harness binds 127.0.0.1; SQLCipher test keys are throwaway; no real customer data; fault tools are never installed on a customer venue PC.
- **Licence**: no test tool is licence-blocked (Hosted-only; no tool is shipped). WinDivert (LGPLv3/GPLv2) and rapid (MPL-2.0) are test-only and not redistributed; if redistribution is ever planned, needs counsel (**closed by founder statement (2026-10-18)**; the licences' own terms still apply to any redistribution). Antithesis terms NOT SOURCEABLE.
- **Threat model**: required (money records) — `docs/architecture/threat-models/local-first-sync.md` not written yet; docs/architecture does not exist (F440).

## 7. Performance & Caching Notes

- **Expected load**: NOT SOURCEABLE. Operations per branch per day, command size and the longest offline period are unknown (F33, F131); the founder's operation-count test on the Glitch server is pending. Shape only: Tier 1 has one device per branch, and writes arrive in bursts after an outage.
- **Hot paths**:
  - the local write plus outbox append, in one transaction on the counter, which never waits on the network (F111, F122);
  - the checkpoint write on the HDD reference PC (S309);
  - the server batch apply: inbox lookup, compare-and-set cursor and business inserts — about 4–6 statements, unmeasured (B);
  - the feed pull: a horizon range scan in one Repeatable Read read-only transaction (F204);
  - bootstrap and catch-up after a long offline period (S5, S206);
  - the day-close recompute.
- **Caching decision inputs** (`caching-standard.md`, gate 5): **no cache** for the inbox, cursor or dedupe record. Written reason: money dedupe must not depend on an evictable store, and a TTL-bound key fails the restored-backup case (B, E). Cache candidates: a per-tenant configuration snapshot keyed by version (C), the verified lease as an in-process memo (D), and entitlement state with a short TTL and explicit invalidation (A). The full decision goes through `/cache-design`.
- **Budgets affected**: `docs/architecture/perf-budgets.md` does not exist, so budgets are "not checked" (F440). Still to set: counter action latency, batch-apply p95, feed-pull p95, queries per request, batch and payload limits, horizon age and checkpoint interval. Spike timings from the Linux dev host are indicative only.
- **Connections and retention**: a push stream holds one connection per device, and `LISTEN` needs a session-mode connection rather than transaction pooling (F207). Inbox rows grow with sales (S108).

### 7.1 Per-part notes (copied unchanged)

#### 7.1.1 Part A

- **Expected load**: NOT SOURCEABLE - operations per branch per day are unknown (brief: the founder's aggregate operation-count test on the Glitch server is still to run). Tier 1 is one cashier device per branch, several possible later.
- **Hot paths**: local write plus outbox/queue append on the cashier thread (must not wait for the network); the server upload handler (idempotent insert, tenant-scoped); initial sync and catch-up after a long offline period (queue size and download size unmeasured: spike S5). Under A, one slow or rejected write blocks all later uploads (F6).
- **Caching candidates**: the local database is itself the client cache of reference data (menu, prices, plans), updated by pull; server side, entitlement/subscription state read per sync is a candidate for a short-TTL cache with explicit invalidation on plan change. Full cache decision belongs in `docs/architecture/cache-designs/` (caching gate 5); a written "no cache" reason is acceptable for the money write path.
- **Budgets affected**: `docs/architecture/perf-budgets.md` was not read in this part; any p95, queries-per-request and payload budget for the upload endpoint is NOT ASSESSED. No engine benchmark was sourced (NOT SOURCEABLE).

#### 7.1.2 Part B

- **Expected load**: NOT SOURCEABLE — no figures (F131). Shape only: one device per branch in Tier 1, writes arrive in bursts after an outage (a backlog of days), not steadily.
- **Hot paths**: the apply transaction (inbox lookup, cursor compare-and-set, business inserts, inbox insert) and the post-outage catch-up of a long backlog; per-device serialisation (F126) means one device's backlog cannot be parallelised but does not block other devices.
- **Caching candidates**: none on the write path. The idempotency record is the database row itself (a cache here could lose dedupe state, B1-B). Reading the cursor is a primary-key lookup. Written cache decision for `caching-standard.md`: no cache for inbox and cursor, reason: correctness of money dedupe must not depend on an evictable store. The responses to the device may carry no cache headers.
- **Budgets affected**: `docs/architecture/perf-budgets.md` figures were not read in this part (F130): p95 latency per command, queries per request (the apply path issues about 4 to 6 statements: unmeasured), payload size. The batch size and payload limits are config values; initial numbers are NOT SOURCEABLE until S105.
- **Retention cost**: inbox rows kept indefinitely grow with sales volume; growth per venue-year is unmeasured (S108). Partitioning by month is a PostgreSQL feature not researched here.

#### 7.1.3 Part C

- **Expected load**: NOT SOURCEABLE - the brief's operation-count test on the Glitch server is still pending; no figures for facts per day or devices per tenant exist. Tier 1 is one device per branch.
- **Hot paths**: the feed pull (range scan on the change log by horizon) and the batch apply (idempotent insert of facts). Both are tenant-scoped under RLS. The horizon read must run in one Repeatable Read read-only transaction (F204). A long transaction anywhere delays all feeds (F208); set transaction and statement timeouts for roles that write change rows.
- **Caching candidates**: configuration snapshot per tenant keyed by version (`{env}:{svc}:cfg:v1:{tenant_id}:{config_version}`), immutable per version, TTL with jitter, singleflight on miss, new version on every write. The feed itself is not cached across tenants; per-device pull responses may carry an ETag. A written cache decision is still needed per `caching-standard.md`; cache down must stay correct.
- **Budgets affected**: `docs/architecture/perf-budgets.md` does not exist in this repository, so no budget was checked ("not checked", not "met"). Budgets to set: feed pull p95, batch apply p95, queries per request, batch size limit, horizon age.
- **Connections**: an SSE or WebSocket stream holds one connection per device; transaction-pooled PgBouncer cannot carry `LISTEN` (F207), so a push relay using `NOTIFY` (F206) needs a direct session connection.

#### 7.1.4 Part D

Budgets in `docs/architecture/perf-budgets.md` were not read for this part: "not checked", never "met". All load figures below are expectations to be confirmed, not sourced.

- **Expected load**: one cashier device per branch in Tier 1 (brief decision log 2026-10-16); per device one lease renewal per lease interval and one checkpoint write every N seconds (N is config, S309). Server load from this part is small; no number is sourced.
- **Hot paths**: (1) billing computation on the counter - integer arithmetic over a few segments, expected microseconds (not benchmarked); (2) the checkpoint write on the HDD reference PC (S309); (3) lease signature verification, done once per lease load, not per sale; (4) sync batch ingest adds one server time read per batch.
- **Caching candidates**: parsed and verified lease held in process memory, invalidated when a higher sequence arrives or when the budget reaches zero (written cache decision to go in the spec or `/cache-design`: pattern in-process memo, key = lease sequence, no TTL beyond the lease, no stampede risk with one device per branch); server side: per-plan grace and rounding parameters and the tenant's IANA zone (read-mostly; invalidate on plan or tenant change); tzdata release pinned in the server image, not cached. Nothing in this part is cached in Valkey/Redis/Dragonfly.
- **Budgets affected**: counter action latency (billing computation and checkpoint must not add measurable delay), sync batch size and queries per request (one insert path for events plus flags; no N+1 when flagging), payload size per event (extra time fields add a few bytes per event).

#### 7.1.5 Part E

- **Expected load**: the spike is a correctness test, not a load test. Tier 1 is one device per branch; per-device action rates and payload sizes are NOT SOURCEABLE — not in the files read.
- **Hot paths** (for production, not the spike): the sync submit endpoint and the idempotency lookup, the local outbox drain, day-close fold.
- **Caching candidates / decision (caching-standard.md)**: No cache is recommended for the idempotency/dedupe record on the sync write path — it is a correctness record that must be in the same database transaction as the ledger effect; a cache could lose or diverge from it. If Redis-protocol idempotency keys (`SET NX` with TTL, per `caching-standard.md`) are proposed later, the TTL must exceed the longest client retry window including a multi-day offline period; the spike's SC-12 (restored backup) shows why a TTL-bound key is unsafe. Full cache decision: Part B / the spec.
- **Budgets affected**: `docs/architecture/perf-budgets.md` does not exist → "not checked" (F440). Any timing the spike reports carries its hardware and conditions; Linux dev host numbers are "indicative only".

## 8. Observability Notes

The parts proposed different names for the same signals. The unified set below is the input to `/observability-plan`. Names are illustrative; semconv 1.44.0 attribute names were not looked up.

- **Spans**:
  - Server: `sync.batch.receive` with child `sync.command.apply` (outcome: applied, duplicate, gap, rejected, payload_mismatch, seq_conflict); `sync.changes.pull` (child: horizon read); `sync.bootstrap`; `lease.issue`; `dayclose.recompute`.
  - Client: `sync.outbox.enqueue`, `sync.outbox.send`, `sync.changes.apply`, `lease.verify`, `session.close.compute`.
  - `traceparent` is propagated from client to server; tenant and device ids are span attributes only.
- **Metrics**:
  - RED per sync endpoint; `sync_commands_total{outcome}`, `sync_batch_size`, `sync_apply_duration_seconds`.
  - Device-reported: `sync_cursor_lag`, `sync_oldest_pending_age_seconds`.
  - Sync health: `sync_quarantine_open`, `sync_seq_conflict_total`, `sync_conflict_flagged_total{class}`, `feed_horizon_age_seconds`, `sync_bootstrap_total{reason}`, `sync_client_unsupported_total{version}`, `push_reconnect_total`.
  - Lease and time: `lease_refresh_total{result}`, `lease_expired_total`, `lease_replay_detected_total`, `clock_anomaly_total{kind}`, `sync_skew_seconds`.
  - **Must stay zero:** `ledger_drift_minor_units`, `billing_amount_mismatch_total`, `dayclose_mismatch_total`.
  - No device, tenant or action id as a metric label.
- **Log events** carry `trace_id` and contain no PII, payloads, tokens or amounts tied to named staff (ASVS V16.2.5):
  - sync: `sync.command.applied`, `sync.command.duplicate`, `sync.command.gap`, `sync.command.rejected`, `sync.device.seq_conflict` (security event), `sync.batch.too_large`, `sync.auth.expired`, `sync.conflict.flagged`, `sync.feed.cursor_too_old`, `sync.bootstrap.started`, `sync.bootstrap.completed`, `sync.client.unsupported_version`;
  - lease and clock: `lease.issued`, `lease.signature_invalid`, `lease.unknown_kid`, `lease.sequence_regressed`, `clock.backward_detected`, `clock.skew_flagged`, `session.elapsed_from_wall_after_restart`;
  - money: `billing.mismatch`, `dayclose.flagged_for_review`.
- **Alerts and runbooks**:
  - Alerts: any non-zero drift or mismatch; a sequence conflict; quarantine age; a lagging or silent device approaching N5 grace; feed horizon age; many devices near lease expiry at once.
  - Runbooks: device diverged or restored, rejected money command review, day close differs, stuck outbox, re-bootstrap, venue locked by N5 expiry, clock anomaly review.
  - `slos.md` does not exist; SLOs belong to `sre-lead`. The POS states offline, syncing, failed and conflict (brief F4) need matching telemetry names.

### 8.1 Per-part notes (copied unchanged)

#### 8.1.1 Part A

Per gate 4 of `coding-standards.md` (OTel span, RED metrics, structured logs with `trace_id`, no PII). The names below are proposals for the observability plan, not existing conventions.

- **Spans**: `sync.upload` (per batch; attributes: batch size, tenant, device ID, outcome), `sync.upload.apply_event` (per event), `sync.pull`/`sync.checkpoint_apply` on the client where traceable, `sync.snapshot_bootstrap`.
- **Metrics (RED / business)**: upload requests, error rate and duration (RED); duplicate-event rate (replays absorbed); upload queue depth and age of the oldest unsent write per device (a growing age is the offline or stalled-queue signal, F6); last successful sync per device; rejected-write count by reason; reconciliation difference (server totals vs device totals) as a business metric.
- **Log events**: `sync.upload.accepted`, `sync.upload.duplicate_ignored`, `sync.upload.rejected` (reason code, no payload amounts tied to a person), `sync.queue.stalled`, `sync.checkpoint.applied`, `sync.device.first_seen`; carry `trace_id`, tenant, device ID. No PII, no secrets, no database key material.
- **Alerts / SLO / runbook needed**: alert on oldest-unsent-write age above a threshold and on any device silent longer than the N5 grace minus a margin; runbook for a stalled device queue (F6) and for duplicate-event spikes. Thresholds NOT SOURCEABLE (to be set with the SLOs).

#### 8.1.2 Part B

Attribute names from OTel semantic conventions (semconv 1.44.0) were not looked up (F130); the names below are the product's own and need mapping by `observability-engineer`.

- **Spans**: `sync.batch.receive` (attributes: device id, batch size, first and last seq, result counts; tenant id as an attribute but never as a metric label); `sync.command.apply` per command (command type, outcome: applied, duplicate, gap, rejected, payload_mismatch, seq_conflict); client side `sync.outbox.send` and `sync.outbox.enqueue` (local transaction). `trace_id` propagated from the device on the request.
- **Metrics (RED / business)**: `sync_commands_total{outcome}`; `sync_batch_duration_seconds`; `sync_batch_size`; `sync_apply_duration_seconds`; gauge `sync_cursor_lag` (device's highest seq minus server cursor, reported by the device); `sync_oldest_pending_age_seconds` per device; `sync_quarantine_open` (rejected commands awaiting review); `sync_seq_conflict_total` (restore or clone suspected); `sync_retry_total` and `sync_auth_expired_total`. No per-device or per-tenant label on counters (cardinality).
- **Log events** (structured, with `trace_id`, no payloads, no amounts tied to named people, no tokens): `sync.command.applied`, `sync.command.duplicate`, `sync.command.gap`, `sync.command.rejected` (reason code), `sync.device.seq_conflict` (security event), `sync.batch.too_large`, `sync.auth.expired`.
- **Alerts / SLO / runbook needed**: alert on `sync_seq_conflict_total > 0`, on quarantine age, and on a device lagging beyond a config threshold; the SLO for "time from sale to visible on the owner dashboard when online" is not yet defined (owner: `sre-lead`, blocks nothing in this note); runbooks: "device flagged as diverged" and "rejected money command review".

#### 8.1.3 Part C

Per `observability-standard.md`: low-cardinality span names, no raw ids, no PII, `trace_id` in logs, `traceparent` propagated; `tenant_id` only where the tenant count is bounded.

- **Spans**: `POST /v1/sync/batch` (children: dedup check, apply per fact type, day-close totals); `GET /v1/sync/changes` (child: horizon read); `GET /v1/sync/bootstrap`; push stream connect; client spans for pull, outbox push and bootstrap, propagating `traceparent`. Endpoint paths are illustrative, the API contract names them.
- **Metrics (RED / business / USE)**: requests, errors and duration per sync endpoint; batch size; facts applied and facts deduplicated (a spike in dedup after an outage is normal, a sudden unexplained rise is a signal); conflicts flagged by class (negative stock, config-version mismatch, dashboard/device overlap, duplicate station owner); day closes flagged for total mismatch; feed horizon age; outbox depth and age (reported by the client, no device id on the label); bootstraps and re-bootstraps by reason; "cursor too old" responses; unsupported-client responses by version; push connections, reconnects, missed heartbeats; DB pool use.
- **Log events** (no PII, no secrets): `sync.batch.applied`, `sync.fact.duplicate_ignored`, `sync.conflict.flagged` (class, internal ids), `sync.day_close.mismatch`, `sync.feed.cursor_too_old`, `sync.bootstrap.started`, `sync.bootstrap.completed`, `sync.client.unsupported_version`.
- **Alerts / SLO / runbook needed**: alert on feed horizon age above a threshold, on a fast rise in dedup or flagged conflicts, and on a device whose outbox age approaches the N5 grace; runbooks for re-bootstrap, a stuck outbox at a venue, and a mismatched day close. The POS states offline, syncing, failed and conflict (brief F4) need matching telemetry names.

#### 8.1.4 Part D

Standard: `.claude/docs/observability-standard.md` (not re-read in detail for this part; names below follow the gate-4 rule: span, RED metrics and structured logs with `trace_id`, no PII).

- **Spans**: `lease.issue` and `lease.verify` (attributes: sequence, `kid`, result, never the token); `session.close.compute` (attributes: elapsed_source, segments, amount_piasters is business data - allowed, no staff name); `sync.batch.ingest` (attributes: skew bucket, anomaly count); `day.close.review` (flag counts).
- **Metrics**: `lease_refresh_total{result}`, `lease_remaining_grace_seconds` (per device, low cardinality via bucketed histogram rather than a label per device), `lease_expired_total`, `lease_replay_detected_total`, `clock_anomaly_total{kind}` (kinds: skew, backward_step, wall_mono_divergence, boot_marker_change, wall_after_restart), `sync_skew_seconds` histogram, `billing_amount_mismatch_total` (device vs server recomputation; expected zero; any increment is a defect), `session_elapsed_source_total{source}`.
- **Log events** (structured, `trace_id`, tenant id, device id; no staff identity, no token body, no PII): `lease.issued`, `lease.signature_invalid`, `lease.unknown_kid`, `lease.sequence_regressed`, `clock.backward_detected`, `clock.skew_flagged`, `session.elapsed_from_wall_after_restart`, `billing.mismatch`, `dayclose.flagged_for_review`. Audit records (P6) carry both times and the actor, kept in the audit store, not in telemetry (ASVS V16.2.2: UTC or explicit offset).
- **Alerts / SLO / runbook needed**: alert on any `billing_amount_mismatch_total` increase; warning when many devices approach lease expiry at once (server outage signal); runbook for "venue locked by N5 expiry" (Q3) and for "clock anomaly review" for support. SLO for lease endpoint availability is a `sre-lead` decision.

#### 8.1.5 Part E

From `observability-standard.md` — what the real sync feature must emit (the spike may log less, but its harness should record the same names so the production tests can reuse the oracle):

- **Spans**: client `sync.submit` (per outbox item), server `POST /v1/sync/actions`-style span (low-cardinality name), `SyncService.Apply`, `Ledger.Commit`, `Idempotency.Lookup`, `DayClose.Recompute`; `traceparent` propagated from client to server; attributes `app.tenant_id`, `app.device_id`, `app.action_kind` (never the action id as a span name).
- **Metrics (RED + business)**: request rate/error/duration on the sync endpoint; outbox depth and oldest-unsent age (client, reported on next sync); `sync_duplicate_total` (replays absorbed), `sync_conflict_total`, `sync_rejected_total{reason}`; ledger conservation drift gauge from a periodic recompute (`ledger_drift_minor_units`, must be 0); day-close mismatch count. No `action_id` or `device_id` as a metric label (cardinality).
- **Log events** (no PII, no payment data — ASVS 16.2.5): `sync.action.applied`, `sync.action.duplicate_ignored`, `sync.action.rejected`, `sync.seq.gap`, `sync.backup_replay_suspected`, `dayclose.mismatch`; fields per the standard (`trace_id`, `tenant_id`, …).
- **Alerts / SLO / runbook needed**: alert on `ledger_drift_minor_units != 0` and on `dayclose.mismatch`; runbook for "device reports old sequence numbers" and "day close differs"; `slos.md` does not exist yet (F440).

| OQ3 | N5: grace length per plan, behaviour at expiry, emergency unlock, backward-at-boot policy D2-P1/P2; reword risk 8 to exclude administrator snapshot restore and app patching. **Answered in part:**<br>- **Lease length (licensing note, founder 2026-10-17):** G = 7 days on the base plan; higher plans longer, set per plan.<br>- **Expiry behaviour (founder, 2026-10-17):** **(1)** the cashier and the owner are warned 24 hours before the offline lease ends; **(2)** at expiry, open sessions can still be closed and paid, so no money is lost; **(3)** no new session and no new sale until the device reaches the server; **(4)** local data stays readable and exportable, and the whole backlog is accepted when the device reconnects. This also answers the licensing note's open question on a lease that runs out mid-shift: new business stops at G, open sessions finish.<br>- **Time source:** the lease is counted on server time plus the device's monotonic counter, never the Windows clock (founder design input in `docs/research/licensing.md` § 5; reuse D1-B, one clock).<br>- **Spike:** SC-25b, mechanics with injected clocks, in phase 3.<br>- **Emergency unlock — founder proposal (2026-10-17), for `/license-design` to design and the licensing threat model to check; not built in this spike:**<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- **When:** the lease has run out and the venue cannot reach the server.<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- **Flow:** the device shows a challenge code; the owner calls Kodelix; the operator issues a short signed unlock code bound to that device and that challenge (single use), which extends the lease by 3 days.<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- **Limits:** at most 3 per device seat per year, each logged and alerting.<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- Routed to `docs/research/licensing.md` (open questions).<br>- **Still open:** emergency unlock design (above); reboots and powered-off time (licensing note part 3); restore of an older state file (the TPM clock question); the risk 8 rewording | B: D2 design, retention rules, threat model | user via `/license-design` (expiry behaviour decided) | before the ADR |

Founder chose device code + never-skipping per-device counter, voids/returns as new documents; server-leased blocks are the fallback. The Law 206/2020 Art. 37 "serial number" reading (needs counsel) is **closed by founder statement (2026-10-18)**: no issue with the founder's choice | B: the "close and pay" command, numbering ADR | user | before the ADR |
| OQ6 | What happens to a permanently rejected money command (accept late, void with reason, escalate)? Principle 1 forbids a silent drop. **Answered (founder, 2026-10-16):** a money fact that happened at the counter is never rejected. The server records it as it happened and flags it "needs review" with the reason (for example a permission revoked earlier, or a price changed). It goes into an owner review queue on the dashboard, where the owner accepts it or issues a correction as a **new** record, never a deletion. Only forged or unbound commands are refused (unknown device, wrong tenant, bad signature), and each refusal is a security event and an owner alert. Still open: the review-queue UX and spec, and whether the server's `payload_mismatch` / `seq_conflict` outcomes (§ 5) become "record and flag" for money facts | B: B3 review workflow and UX (rule decided; spec and UX open) | user (decided), `product-manager`, `ux-designer` | rule decided; spec before the ADR |
| OQ7 | Restore/clone policy on a sequence conflict: stop the device, flag and continue, or re-register? **Answered (founder, 2026-10-17; to be confirmed in the sync threat model):**<br>- The counter keeps selling locally.<br>- Sync for that device identity is quarantined.<br>- Every money command from both copies is stored and none is dropped (as OQ6).<br>- The owner is alerted and chooses in the dashboard which copy is the real device.<br>Spike: SC-13 (cloned disk) and TS-01a test this policy in phase 3.<br>**SC-13 + TS-01a dev run (2026-10-18, tier E, spike README):** a second machine id under one identity (clone) or a reused sequence number from the same machine (rollback with the marker restored too) quarantines the identity. Every command from every copy is stored, nothing reaches the ledger, one owner alert, and every counter keeps selling. On the owner's choice the kept copy continues the identity, and every other copy and any overwritten timeline are applied into retired streams and the owner's review queue. No sale is lost or doubled. Finding P3-F11: before this policy, a rollback that reused synced numbers lost the restored device's sales. **Decided (founder, 2026-10-18):** (a) review-queue sales count in revenue and day-close totals at once, because the cash was taken and totals must match the drawer. They show on their own labelled line ("of which X EGP from a retired copy, pending your review") until the owner reviews them. (b) During quarantine the dashboard shows "held: N sales, X EGP" for that device, with the alert. Spike checks R52, R53. | B: B3 detection design (policy decided; confirmation at the threat model) | user (decided), `security-lead` | sync `/threat-model` |
| OQ8 | Billing rules: minimum minutes, increment, rounding, per-segment vs total, which segment pays the rounding (idea-pool S3, N4), tax order; billing while the PC is off; idea-pool S7 backdate limits and offline approver; business-day cut-off W4. **Answered in part (founder, 2026-10-17):**<br>(1) Minimum billable minutes, the billing increment and money rounding are **owner settings**; no fixed values in code.<br>(2) A session with several segments (tier change, station move) is **rounded once on the total, never per segment**.<br>(3) If the cashier PC is off while a session runs, the time keeps counting, and the owner sees a "device was off from X to Y" notice when it restarts.<br>(4) **Replaced (founder, 2026-10-17):** there is **no cut-off hour**. The business day is opened and closed by an explicit **End Day** action, independent of the calendar day. **End Day is refused while any bill, session or shift is open**, so a session and its bill always belong to the same business day. An earlier proposal (End Day allowed with open sessions carried over) is superseded (founder confirmed, 2026-10-18).<br>(5) Tax order waits for the ETA reply and the tax advisor. Until then spike phase 5 uses a configurable inclusive/exclusive setting (founder, 2026-10-18).<br>(6) **(founder, 2026-10-17)** Backdate limits (idea-pool S7) are an **owner setting** in the dashboard; no fixed values in code.<br>Spike: SC-23 rounds once on the total, using the owner's `round_piasters` step from the price list. Minimum and increment are not exercised (billing spec). The device-off notice is SC-30; End Day is SC-31.<br>**SC-30 dev run (2026-10-18, tier E, spike README, injected clock):** session time keeps counting across power-offs and app kills (anchored monotonic time plus the wall-clock gap from the last-alive checkpoint). Every session is billed its true elapsed time, and the owner gets one "device was off from X to Y" notice per outage. A kill inside the outage write, or a crash before the first timer tick, changes neither. The checkpoint must be refreshed in the start-up transaction. **Decided (founder, 2026-10-18):** (a) every outage longer than a minute stays visible. With open sessions it is an owner notice at once (money affected); with none it goes into the owner's daily summary only, because a PC switched off in business hours with nothing recorded is a known way to take cash off the books. (b) "PC was off" (boot id changed) and "app was closed while the PC was on" (same boot id) are shown separately, and the second is marked as the more suspicious. The boot-id signal on Windows is still to be confirmed in phase 4 (S301/S302).<br>**SC-31 dev run (2026-10-18, tier E, spike README):** End Day is refused while a bill, session or shift is open, naming each one. The business day is a device counter carried by every bill. The End Day command (day, bill count, total) travels in the device sequence; the server recomputes the day from its own bills and agrees in every run, including offline days and a kill during End Day (closed with its command, or still open; never half). A bill for an already closed day is flagged. | B: billing function, session spec | user (rules 1–4 and 6 decided), `product-manager`, `pricing-billing-designer` | billing spec |
| OQ9 | Stock: negative allowed (flag only), or refuse the sale for some items? May a dashboard edit touch a bill or session the device is changing? **Answered (founder, 2026-10-17):**<br>(1) Per item, the owner chooses "allow negative stock and alert" or "refuse the sale". New items default to allow and alert.<br>(2) The owner may edit an open bill or session from the dashboard **only while the device is connected**. The edit is sent to the device as a command, the device applies it and confirms, and the dashboard shows "pending" until then. If the device is offline the dashboard refuses the edit. If the bill closed first, the edit is rejected visibly.<br>(3) A closed bill is changed only by a correction command (void or adjustment) that keeps the original. Once an e-receipt exists, the correction is an ETA Return Receipt.<br>Spike: SC-28 (dashboard edit of an open bill) and SC-29 (stock policy per item) in phase 3.<br>**SC-28 dev run (2026-10-18, tier E, spike README P3-F10):** "pending, never stuck" holds in two parts. An edit **never delivered** is ended by the server at its deadline and never sent afterwards. An edit **delivered once** can be ended only by the device, at its next contact, because the reply may have arrived. Until then the dashboard must say "sent to the device at X, waiting for it". A device that goes offline right after receiving an edit keeps it open until it reconnects.<br>**Decided (founder, 2026-10-18), for the dashboard spec:** (a) P3-F10 is acceptable. Until the device confirms, the dashboard shows **"sent to the device at X, waiting for it"**, and it **never shows "rejected" for a delivered edit**. (b) While an edit to a bill has been delivered and is not yet confirmed, the dashboard **refuses a second edit to the same bill**.<br>**SC-29 dev run (2026-10-18, tier E, spike README):** the device decides from the policy it holds. A "refuse" item without stock refuses the whole sale, naming the item, and nothing is billed. A new item behaves as allow-and-alert. A policy change applies when the device receives it. Device stock, server mirror and the model agree, including across a server restore and a duplicate resend. **Decided (founder, 2026-10-18):** (a) the owner is alerted when an item's stock crosses from zero or above to below zero. There is no alert on further negative sales, and the next alert comes only after a restock brings it back to zero or above and it crosses again. (b) Sales under a previous policy are folded into the SC-23 owner summary, not a separate note. There is one summary per period in which the device lacked the current config, listing both old-price and old-policy sales, judged by cfg_version as in SC-23 (spike check R40). | B: C1 stock and bill policy (decided) | user (decided), `product-manager` | spec |
